<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove convergence of thermal FK–Ising interfaces, for every fixed nonzero mass of either sign, on uniformly angle-bounded isoradial lattices in bounded simply connected domains. The limit is independent of the lattice and the admissible domain approximation. For positive mass it is the unique massive SLE$`_{16/3}`$ law with locally finite-energy drift prescribed by a massive boundary value problem; negative mass follows by duality and reversal. The theorem also allows Carathéodory approximations whose diameters diverge.

- **claim-main-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $(\Omega,a,b,m)$ as above, there is a curve law $\mu_{\Omega,a,b,m}$ such that, for every sequence with mesh tending to zero and satisfying all the preceding domain, marked Carathéodory, angle and nome assumptions, $$\mathcal L(\chi_\delta\circ\gamma_\delta) \ \Longrightarrow\ \chi_*\mu_{\Omega,a,b,m}$$ in the metric <a href="#mod:curve-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:curve-metric">[mod:curve-metric]</a>, along the full sequence. The physical prime-end curve law $\mu_{\Omega,a,b,m}$ is independent of the lattices, admissible domain approximations and nome sequence.* *For $m>0$, $\mu_{\Omega,a,b,m}$ is the unique law in Definition <a href="#def:law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:law">3</a>. For $m<0$, it is the reversal of the positive-mass law in $(\Omega,b,a)$ with mass $\|m\|$, with primal and dual boundary labels interchanged. In either case we call it thermal massive $\mathrm{SLE}_{16/3}$.
  - Explanation: For every admissible marked domain and mass there is one curve law that is the full-sequence limit of the transferred discrete interfaces. The law does not depend on the lattices, the domain approximations or the nome sequence. For positive mass it is the unique law of the continuum definition; for negative mass it is the reversal of the dual positive-mass law.
- **claim-bvp-unique** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-bvp-unique)): Equation <a href="#mod:bvp" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:bvp">[mod:bvp]</a> has exactly one solution in this class. It satisfies $0\le h\le1$. On interior compact sets, $h$ and its first derivatives depend continuously on the marked domain in the Carathéodory topology, when the domains lie in a fixed bounded physical region.
  - Explanation: The nonlinear scalar boundary value problem has exactly one bounded classical solution. This solution lies between the two boundary values and depends continuously on the marked domain on interior compact sets.
- **claim-inputs-observable-crossings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-inputs)): Consider the weights of Section <a href="#mod:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:section">[mod:section]</a>, with $r_\delta/\delta\to m/2$, on isoradial meshes whose rhombus half-angles belong to $[\eta,\pi/2-\eta]$.* 1. *For marked Carathéodory convergence to a bounded simply connected two-point domain, the reconstructed two-point FK fermion converges locally uniformly, up to its common sign, to a smooth massive-holomorphic function $f$: $$\bar\partial f+\mathrm im\overline f=0.$$ Its square integral $h$, normalized by its two boundary values, is the unique bounded solution $$\Delta h=-4m\|\nabla h\|,\qquad h=1\ \hbox{on the wired bank},\qquad h=0\ \hbox{on the dual-wired bank}.$$ The boundary values are prime-end limits away from the two marked ends, and $0\le h\le1$. The convergence includes uniform interior bounds on protected compact subsets.* 2. *For every fixed rectangle aspect ratio and every finite physical scale bound, the crossing probabilities of both colors are bounded away from zero, uniformly over all boundary conditions and all nondegenerate lattice rectangles below that scale whose side lengths exceed a fixed multiple of the mesh. The scale constants are independent of the mesh.
  - Explanation: The two imported inputs: the discrete two-point fermion converges to a massive-holomorphic function whose square integral solves the scalar problem, and rectangle crossing probabilities of both colors are bounded below uniformly at bounded physical scales.
- **claim-domain-markov** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-domain-markov)): After any finite sequence of these revelations, the unqueried continuing problem is an isoradial Dobrushin problem with the same interior weights. Its two banks are the old banks extended by the revealed diagonals. All discrepancies between the geometric slit and this graph domain occur in a bounded number of cell layers along the slit, together with components cut off from the continuation. The conditional probabilities of the two outcomes of a genuine new pairing are uniformly bounded below.
  - Explanation: After finitely many local revelations along the interface, the unexplored continuation is again an isoradial Dobrushin problem with the same interior weights, and both new local outcomes keep conditional probability bounded below.
- **claim-arm-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-geo-arm)): Let $A=A(z;r,R)$ be a physical annulus with $R\le L_1$, where $L_1$ is fixed. Inside $A$, let $\mathcal V$ be a union of regions whose accessible noncircular banks all have one color, and let $c$ be the other color. Circular ends may carry arbitrary induced boundary identifications. After buffering the two ends by a fixed number of cell layers, the probability of an actual $c$-arm crossing $A$ in any of the regions is at most $$K\left(\frac{r\vee\delta}{R}\right)^\alpha. \label{geo:arm-bound}$$ The constants are independent of the number of regions and remain valid after arbitrary conditioning outside buffered shells. For specified disjoint buffered annuli the corresponding upper bounds can be multiplied.
  - Explanation: For a union of regions whose accessible noncircular banks all have one color, the probability of an actual arm of the other color crossing the annulus obeys a power-law bound, independent of the number of regions and of outside conditioning.
- **claim-g2-unforced-crossings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-g2)): The regularly drawn medial interfaces in domains of diameter at most $L$ satisfy condition G2 of [[KS](#ref-KS)], uniformly over the allowed meshes, domains, and thermal parameters.
  - Explanation: The drawn medial interfaces satisfy the conditional unforced-annulus crossing condition of the external curve theory, uniformly over meshes, domains and thermal parameters.
- **claim-small-sets-normalized-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-crosscut)): Let $V_n$ be simply connected planar domains containing $z_0$, and let $\chi_n:V_n\to\mathbb D$ satisfy $\chi_n(z_0)=0$ and the same angular normalization. Suppose $$0<c_0\le \operatorname{crad}_{V_n}(z_0)\le C_0<\infty.$$ If $E_n\subset V_n$ is connected and $\operatorname{diam}E_n\to0$, then $\operatorname{diam}\chi_n(E_n)\to0$. The same conclusion holds after adjoining accessible prime-end endpoints approached along $E_n$. In particular, bounded-cell changes of a mesh tending to zero have vanishing diameter in the normalized disc, provided their incident sectors are respected.
  - Explanation: Connected interior sets of vanishing physical diameter have vanishing diameter in normalized disc coordinates, given a lower bound on conformal radius, with no dependence on area or diameter of the domain.
- **claim-joint-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-compactness)): Under the common diameter bound, the laws of the interfaces transferred by their normalized maps to the closed disc are tight in the oriented curve metric. Every subsequential limit is a chordal Loewner trace with a continuous driving function and strict capacity growth. It reaches the target only at the end and is continuously completed in disc coordinates. Along a further coupling, the curves and their half-plane drivers converge jointly, with uniform convergence of the drivers and capacity-parametrized curves on every finite capacity interval.
  - Explanation: The transferred interface laws are tight, and subsequential limits are chordal Loewner traces with continuous drivers and strict capacity growth; curves and drivers converge jointly.
- **claim-active-domain-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-active)): In a coupling as in Proposition <a href="#geo:compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:compactness">11</a>, let $t_n\to t<\infty$. The active graph domains at the corresponding exploration times converge in the marked Carathéodory sense to $O_t$, locally from any protected point in $O_t$. Their moving corner converges to the prime end represented by $W_t$. The assertion includes contacts of the limiting trace with itself or with an original bank.
  - Explanation: Active graph domains at corresponding exploration times converge in the marked Carathéodory sense to the continuum active domain, and the moving corner converges to the prime end given by the driver.
- **claim-limit-restart-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-restart)): Every limit of Proposition <a href="#geo:compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:compactness">11</a> inherits the conditional crossing estimates needed below in its natural driving filtration. They hold after a live stopping time and after localization on a finite interval on which the relevant Loewner coordinates remain nondegenerate. In the following two estimates, reset capacity time to zero at the chosen live stopping time.* 1. *In the current half-plane chart, put the tip at $0$, rescale a given live point to modulus $1$, and write $x$ for its real part. If its imaginary part is small, so $x$ is near $1$ or $-1$, then $$\mathbb P\{\text{the future trace visits }B(x,d)\mid\text{past}\} \le Kd^\alpha$$ for all sufficiently small $d$.* 2. *For a live physical point $w$, $$\mathbb P\left\{ \inf_{s\text{ before swallowing}} \operatorname{crad}_{O_s}(w) <\varepsilon\,\operatorname{crad}_{O_0}(w) \,\middle\|\,\text{past at the restart}\right\} \le K\varepsilon^\alpha .$$* *The assertions apply in particular at inverse elapsed-clock times and at first angular-level crossings used in Section <a href="#eng:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eng:section">4</a>. No semimartingale property of the driver is assumed here.
  - Explanation: Limit curves inherit the conditional boundary-visit and conformal-radius estimates in the natural driver filtration, including at live stopping times.
- **claim-fermion-martingale** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-martingale)): Every subsequential curve law of Proposition <a href="#geo:compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:compactness">11</a> has a joint realization with a complex field $M_t(w)$, $w\in O_t$, such that $$M_t(w)^2=D h_t(w),\qquad \bar\partial M_t+\mathrm im\overline{M_t}=0.$$ The field is continuous locally in space and time before swallowing, and is smooth in its spatial variable. On every protected compact subset of the live domain, after localization, $M_t(w)$ is a continuous martingale in the joint natural filtration of the curve and field. In particular it is a continuous local martingale at each fixed point before its swallowing time.
  - Explanation: Each subsequential law has a joint realization with a signed continuum fermion field whose square is the derivative of the scalar solution and which is a continuous local martingale on the live domain.
- **claim-moving-mark-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-eng-energy)): For $$\label{eng:J-definition} J_t=\int_{\mathbb H}\frac{\mu_t(v)}{\|v\|^2}\,\mathrm dA(v),$$ there is a constant $K<\infty$ such that $$\label{eng:energy-bound} \mathbb E\int_0^\infty J_t^2\,\mathrm dt \le K m^2\operatorname{area}(\Omega)<\infty.$$ Consequently $J_t$ is finite for almost every capacity time, almost surely.
  - Explanation: The expected integrated square of the moving-mark energy is finite and controlled by the physical area, so the energy is finite for almost every capacity time.
- **claim-point-clock-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eng-clock)): Before the point’s lifetime, $$\label{eng:clock-identities} Y_s=Y_0e^{-2s},\qquad \mathrm d\log\operatorname{crad}_{O_{t(s)}}(w)=-4X_s^2\,\mathrm ds, \qquad \mathrm dt=r_s^2\,\mathrm ds.$$ The same identities hold after any live stopping time, with elapsed capacity and clock times and the corresponding starting values.
  - Explanation: Deterministic identities give exponential decay of the imaginary coordinate in the point clock, the logarithmic rate of the conformal radius, and the capacity increment in clock time.
- **claim-restarted-geometric-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-eng-restarts)): There are $a>0$, $K<\infty$, and $d_*>0$ with the following properties. They hold conditionally on the past at every stopping time $\tau$ on the event $w\in O_\tau$.* 1. *Translate and dilate the current half-plane chart so that its tip is $0$, its target is $\infty$, and $\|Z_\tau\|=1$. If $X_\tau\le d_*$ and $x=\operatorname{Re}(Z_\tau/\|Z_\tau\|)$, then, for $0<d<d_*$, the probability that the restarted trace visits $B(x,d)$ is at most $Kd^a$.* 2. *With $R=\operatorname{crad}_{O_\tau}(w)$ and $0<\varepsilon<1$, $$\label{eng:bulk-crad} \mathbb P\left\{ \inf_{\tau\le t<T_w}\operatorname{crad}_{O_t}(w)<\varepsilon R \,\middle\|\,\mathcal F_\tau\right\} \le K\varepsilon^a.$$ The infimum in this formula is strictly before the lifetime. Conformal radius is not assigned the value zero upon swallowing.* *These estimates remain valid for the inverse-clock stopping times and the first angular-level stopping times used below.
  - Explanation: Conditionally on the past at every stopping time there are power-law bounds for the future trace visiting a small ball near the boundary and for a drop of conformal radius.
- **claim-reflected-ball** (bound, [source](#stmt-eng-reflection)): Consider a chordal Loewner trace in $\mathbb H$ with continuous driver, starting from $0$. Let its maps be $l_t$, so that $l_0$ is the identity. Start a point at $z_0=x+\mathrm iX_0$ with $\|z_0\|=1$. There are absolute constants $c_0>0$ and $K<\infty$ such that, if $0<d<1/8$, $X_0\le c_0d$, and the trace avoids $B(x,d)$, then up to the point’s lifetime $$\label{eng:avoidance-deterministic} \sup_s\frac{X_s}{X_0}\le\frac Kd, \qquad \int_0^{\sigma_*}\frac{X_s}{X_0}\,\mathrm ds\le\frac Kd.$$ Consequently $$\label{eng:avoidance-square} \int_0^{\sigma_*}\left(\frac{X_s}{X_0}\right)^2\mathrm ds \le\frac K{d^2}.$$
  - Explanation: If the trace avoids a ball near the starting point, the angular coordinate and its integrated first and second powers stay controlled by negative powers of the ball radius.
- **claim-angular-controls** (bound, [source](#stmt-eng-controls)): At every live restart, use elapsed point clock and write $X_0$ for its starting angular coordinate. There are $a>0$ and $K<\infty$ such that $$\label{eng:supremum-tail} \mathbb P\left\{\sup_{s\ge0}\frac{X_s}{X_0}>u \,\middle\|\,\text{past}\right\}\le Ku^{-a},\qquad u\ge1.$$ Moreover the conditional laws of $$\label{eng:occupation} I=\int_0^\infty\left(\frac{X_s}{X_0}\right)^2\mathrm ds$$ are uniformly tight, over all live restarts and all starting angles.
  - Explanation: The supremum of the angular coordinate relative to its start has a power-law tail and its squared occupation integral is uniformly tight over restarts.
- **claim-small-power-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-eng-decay)): There exist $p\in(0,1/4)$, $b>0$, and $K<\infty$ such that, from every live restart, $$\label{eng:moment-decay} \mathbb E[X_s^p\mid\text{past}]\le K X_0^p e^{-bs},\qquad s\ge0.$$ The same choice of $p$ has a uniformly bounded conditional $2p$-moment of $\sup_{s\ge0}X_s/X_0$.
  - Explanation: A small fractional power of the angular coordinate contracts exponentially in the point clock from every live restart, and the supremum has a bounded moment of twice that power.
- **claim-weighted-clock-integral** (bound, [source](#stmt-eng-weighted)): For the $p$ in Lemma <a href="#eng:decay" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eng:decay">21</a>, there are $\epsilon>0$ and $K<\infty$ such that, uniformly over every initial live point, $$\label{eng:weighted-clock} \mathbb E\int_0^{\sigma_*}X_s^{2p} \left[ \left(\frac{\sqrt{t(s)}}{r_s}\right)^\epsilon +\left(\frac{r_s}{\sqrt{t(s)}}\right)^\epsilon \right]\mathrm ds\le K.$$ Here $t(s)$ is capacity elapsed from the chosen starting instant. The value at $s=0$ is immaterial.
  - Explanation: The expected clock integral weighted by the small power of the angular coordinate and by both capacity-to-clock ratios is uniformly bounded over every initial live point.
- **claim-driver-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cal-driver)): The subsequential driving process is a continuous semimartingale and, in the above joint filtration, satisfies $$\mathrm dW_t=\sqrt{\frac{16}{3}}\,\mathrm dB_t+ \frac{2\pi}{3}C_t\,\mathrm dt, \qquad \int_0^T C_t^2\,\mathrm dt<\infty\quad\text{almost surely for every }T<\infty, \label{cal:driver-equation}$$ where $B$ is a Brownian motion and $$C_t=\frac4\pi\int_{\mathbb H}P(v)\mu_t(v) \|\nabla(h_t\circ\psi_t)(v)\|\,\mathrm dA(v). \label{cal:C-definition}$$ In particular, neither absolute continuity of the quadratic variation nor absolute continuity of the finite-variation part of $W$ is an assumption of the argument.
  - Explanation: The subsequential driver is a continuous semimartingale with Brownian part and a finite-energy drift given by the weighted gradient integral of the scalar solution.
- **claim-potential-representation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cal-potential)): There is a constant $K$, depending only on $m$ and a physical diameter bound, such that on every active domain $$\begin{aligned} h_t(z)&=u_t(z)+4m\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)\|M_t(w)\|^2\,\mathrm dA(w), \label{cal:representation}\\ \|M_t(w)\|^2&\leq \frac{K}{\operatorname{crad}_{O_t}(w)}. \label{cal:bulk-bound} \end{aligned}$$ In particular the nonnegative potential $$Q_t(z):=\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)\|M_t(w)\|^2\,\mathrm dA(w) \quad\text{satisfies}\quad 0\leq Q_t(z)\leq\frac1{4m}. \label{cal:bounded-potential}$$
  - Explanation: The scalar solution equals the angular harmonic function plus a bounded Green potential of the squared fermion, which obeys a bulk bound in terms of conformal radius.
- **claim-stochastic-green-potential** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cal-stochastic-potential)): On every such stopped interval $[0,T]$, $$\mathbb E\int_0^T\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w) \,\mathrm d[M(w),\overline{M(w)}]_t\,\mathrm dA(w)<\infty. \label{cal:weighted-bracket}$$ The potential $Q_t(z)$ has a continuous semimartingale version, and $$\begin{aligned} \mathrm dQ_t(z)={}&-\frac2\pi P_t(z) \left(\int_{O_t}P_t(w)\|M_t(w)\|^2\,\mathrm dA(w)\right)\mathrm dt \notag\\ &+\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)\,\mathrm d[M(w),\overline{M(w)}]_t\,\mathrm dA(w) \notag\\ &+2\operatorname{Re}\int_{O_t} G_t(z,w)\overline{M_t(w)}\,\mathrm dM_t(w)\,\mathrm dA(w). \label{cal:potential-differential} \end{aligned}$$ The last line is defined by the weighted bracket-isometry limit of compactly supported stochastic integrals. The statement holds simultaneously for countably many protected spatial points, and also as a spatial distribution identity on protected open sets.
  - Explanation: The Green potential has a continuous semimartingale version with an explicit differential consisting of a kernel-loss term, a bracket term and a stochastic integral, without a concentrated term at swallowing times.
- **claim-driver-semimartingale** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cal-recovery)): The continuous process $W$ is a semimartingale in the joint filtration.
  - Explanation: The driver is a semimartingale in the joint filtration, recovered from differences of the potential at two protected points via the implicit function theorem.
- **claim-covariance-clock** (construction, [source](#stmt-cal-covariance)): Locally in time there is a continuous increasing process $L$ such that $\mathrm dt$, $\|\mathrm dV\|$, $\mathrm d[W^{\mathrm M}]$, and all the spatial bracket measures used below are absolutely continuous with respect to $\mathrm dL$. Outside one null set for $\mathrm dL\,\mathrm d\mathbb P$, write $$\tau=\frac{\mathrm dt}{\mathrm dL},\qquad \nu=\frac{\mathrm dV}{\mathrm dL},\qquad U=\dot W^{\mathrm M},\qquad A_{\mathrm{phys}}(w)=\dot M(w). \label{cal:clock-notation}$$ The dots denote covariance vectors with respect to $\mathrm dL$, not time derivatives. These vectors lie in a real Hilbert space, complexified for $A_{\mathrm{phys}}$. Their scalar products are the corresponding bracket densities. The product is extended bilinearly, so $A\cdot\overline A=\|A\|^2$ and $U\cdot U=\|U\|^2$. The field $A_{\mathrm{phys}}$ is spatially smooth on every compact subset of the live domain. Spatial evaluations, derivatives, compact tests, and the Green stochastic integrals in <a href="#cal:potential-differential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:potential-differential">[cal:potential-differential]</a> have their covariance vectors on this same full set. In particular, for protected $z$, $$\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)\|A_{\mathrm{phys}}(w)\|^2\,\mathrm dA(w)<\infty \quad\text{for }\mathrm dL\text{-almost every live time.} \label{cal:clock-green-integrability}$$
  - Explanation: A single continuous increasing clock dominates time, the drift variation, the driver bracket and all spatial bracket measures, giving covariance vectors in a Hilbert space with a Green integrability property.
- **claim-tip-expansions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cal-tip-expansions)): In the current conformal setting, assume <a href="#cal:mu-L2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:mu-L2">[cal:mu-L2]</a>, <a href="#cal:weighted-A" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:weighted-A">[cal:weighted-A]</a>, and the bulk bound $\|F(v)\|^2\leq K/\operatorname{Im}v$ from <a href="#cal:poisson-control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:poisson-control">[cal:poisson-control]</a>. Then <a href="#cal:identity-zero" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:identity-zero">[cal:identity-zero]</a>–<a href="#cal:identity-drift" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:identity-drift">[cal:identity-drift]</a> imply $$\begin{aligned} F(z)^2&=\frac1{\pi z}+o(s^{-1}) \qquad\text{uniformly on }B_s, \label{cal:tip-leading}\\ 2F(z)A(z)&=\frac U{\pi z^2}+o(s^{-3/2}) \qquad\text{in }L^2_{\rm av}(B_s), \label{cal:tip-mixed-weak}\\ \int_{\mathbb H}DG(z,v)\mu(v)\|A(v)\|^2\,\mathrm dA(v) &=o(s^{-2}) \qquad\text{in }L^1_{\rm av}(B_s). \label{cal:tip-bracket} \end{aligned}$$ If in addition $J=\int_{\mathbb H}\mu(v)\|v\|^{-2}\,\mathrm dA(v)<\infty$, then $$\begin{aligned} F(z)^2&=\frac1{\pi z}+C+o(1) \qquad\text{uniformly on }B_s, \label{cal:tip-constant}\\ 2F(z)A(z)&=\frac U{\pi z^2}+o(s^{-1}) \qquad\text{in }L^2_{\rm av}(B_s). \label{cal:tip-mixed-strong} \end{aligned}$$ No finiteness of $J$ is required for <a href="#cal:tip-leading" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:tip-leading">[cal:tip-leading]</a>–<a href="#cal:tip-bracket" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:tip-bracket">[cal:tip-bracket]</a>.
  - Explanation: Averaged expansions of the squared fermion, the mixed term and the bracket potential hold near the moving mark, with a refined constant term when the energy integral is finite.
- **claim-measurable-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-uni-measurable)): There is a fixed nonanticipating progressively measurable extension of $C_t$ to continuous driving paths, agreeing with Equation <a href="#mod:C" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:C">[mod:C]</a> whenever its integral is finite. The same extension can be used for every candidate law in Definition <a href="#def:law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:law">3</a>.
  - Explanation: The drift coefficient admits a fixed nonanticipating progressively measurable extension to continuous driving paths that agrees with the defining integral whenever it is finite.
- **claim-weak-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-uni-weak)): Let $\kappa>0$, and let $b(t,x)$ be a fixed progressively measurable nonanticipating real functional on continuous paths starting at zero. There is at most one law of a continuous weak solution of $$\label{uni:generic-SDE} \mathrm dX_t=\sqrt\kappa\,\mathrm dB_t+b(t,X)\mathrm dt, \qquad X_0=0,$$ among solutions satisfying $$\label{uni:generic-energy} \int_0^T b(t,X)^2\mathrm dt<\infty\quad\hbox{almost surely}$$ for every finite $T$.
  - Explanation: Under a progressively measurable nonanticipating drift functional with almost surely finite energy on finite horizons, the stochastic differential equation has at most one weak solution in law.
- **claim-bounded-uniqueness-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-uni-bounded)): Definition <a href="#def:law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:law">3</a> determines exactly one law. Every admissible approximation whose physical domains have a common diameter bound converges to this law in the disc curve topology.
  - Explanation: The continuum definition determines exactly one law, and every admissible approximation with a common diameter bound converges to it in the disc curve topology.
- **claim-remote-shadows** (bound, [source](#stmt-loc-remote)): For every connected interior arc $E_n\subset\Omega_n\setminus B(0,S-1)$, its disc image has diameter tending to zero, uniformly over the choice of arc. The same is true for the noncentral shadow of a crosscut contained there.* *More precisely, fix a point $\xi\in\partial\mathbb D$, a positive parameter tolerance $a$, and a positive physical tolerance $\rho$. For all sufficiently large $n$, one can choose $s_n$ in a fixed interval $[s_-,s_+]\subset(0,a)$ such that the entire preimage of $\partial B(\xi,s_n)\cap\mathbb D$ is contained in $B(0,S-2)$ and has length at most $\rho$. The endpoints and shadows are understood in the prime-end compactification. The conclusion holds simultaneously for finitely many specified $\xi$’s, with disjoint parameter caps when those points are distinct.
  - Explanation: Connected remote arcs and the shadows of remote crosscuts have vanishing diameter in the disc, and short preimage circles can be selected around finitely many prescribed boundary points.
- **claim-small-attached-obstructions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-obstructions)): Let $V_n\subset\mathbb D$ be the simply connected component containing $0$ after removing finitely many closed, piecewise analytic obstructions and filling the other components. Assume that the obstructions lie within distance $\varepsilon_n\to0$ of $\partial\mathbb D$, and that every point on an obstruction can be joined to $\mathbb C\setminus\mathbb D$ within the removed set by a connected set of diameter at most $\varepsilon_n$. Then the normalized maps $F_n:\mathbb D\to V_n$ satisfy $$\sup_{\overline\mathbb D}\|F_n(z)-z\|\longrightarrow0. \label{loc:maps-identity}$$ For every prime-end occurrence $w=F_n(\zeta)$, this also gives $\|F_n^{-1}(w)-w\|\to0$, uniformly. The same conclusion holds for any number of such obstructions; no bound on their number or total length is required.
  - Explanation: Removing small attached obstructions and filling the discarded components changes the normalized conformal map only by a uniformly vanishing amount, regardless of how many obstructions there are.
- **claim-visible-gates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-gates)): Let $U_{n,j}$ be the component of $\Omega_n\cap B(0,s_j)$ containing $0$. Its visible exit arcs on $\partial B(0,s_j)$ are crosscuts of the initial domain, with pairwise disjoint noncentral shadows. Every point of $\Omega_n$ outside that circle lies behind one mandatory visible gate: every path from $0$ to that point crosses it. A gate at a later level has one specified ancestor gate at every earlier level.* *Call a gate at level $j$ large when its length is at least $2^{-j}$. There are at most $K_S2^j$ large gates at that level. A chain of small ancestors at levels $j$ gives disjoint annuli centered on those ancestors, with inner radii $K2^{-j}$ and outer radii $c_0(j+2)^{-2}$, for all sufficiently large $j$. Every path from a circle-$S$ portal to a point of radius $S+2$, continued to $0$ within $B(0,S)$, crosses these annuli.
  - Explanation: Visible exit arcs of a radial level form a tree of crosscuts with disjoint shadows; every distant point is behind a mandatory gate, large gates are few, and small gates have disjoint surrounding annuli.
- **claim-endpoint-explorations** (construction, [source](#stmt-loc-heads)): For every sufficiently small parameter tolerance $a>0$, the two ends can be explored, each stopped before its first unpeeled cell on a protected fence, with the following properties:* 1. *Each explored head has disc diameter at most $Ka+o(1)$. The two heads lie in disjoint endpoint neighborhoods.* 2. *The center has unqueried connected access chains to the two resulting front corners. These chains lie in $B(0,S-1)$ and on the inner side of every proposed small $H$-cap and of the circle at $S$.* 3. *Conditional on these local revelations, the connecting problem is an ordinary Dobrushin problem. In the initial disc its changes are attached obstructions of diameter at most $Ka+o(1)$, with fillings behind them.* *The preimages of the $H$-cap cuts can subsequently be chosen to have any prescribed small physical diameter $\rho>0$, while retaining these properties and containing the large gates at levels $j\le J$.
  - Explanation: The two endpoints can be explored up to protected fences so that each explored head is small in the disc and the remaining problem is an ordinary Dobrushin problem with small attached obstructions.
- **claim-exact-shielding** (construction, [source](#stmt-loc-shield)): Let a distal cap have boundary color $c$, with its portal strictly inside the physical cutoff $S+3$. There is a finite edge exploration on its distal side with the following alternatives.* 1. *On success, all retained parts of the cap have radius less than $S+3+K\delta_n$. The new retained boundary is made of $c$-open diagonals attached to the original $c$-bank. Every queried edge lies outside or on that boundary, so the retained connecting problem has its ordinary Dobrushin conditional law.* 2. *Failure implies an actual opposite-color path from radius at least $S+3-K\delta_n$ to cells incident to the portal, with an endpoint error of at most $K\delta_n$.* *The same statements hold for all caps explored in a fixed order, stopping the entire surgery at its first failure. Conditioning on the complete successful revelation record imposes no further event on the unqueried central edges.
  - Explanation: A finite edge exploration on the distal side of a monochromatic cap either succeeds, leaving a retained problem with its ordinary conditional law, or fails with an actual opposite-color path.
- **claim-gate-product-failure-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-loc-failure)): With the first $J$ levels and their finite set $H$ fixed, let all $H$-cut preimages have diameter at most $\rho$. Uniformly conditional on the two explored heads, the probability that the shielding procedure fails is bounded by $$\begin{aligned} &K\|H\|\rho^\alpha\\ &\quad+K\sum_{k>J}2^k \prod_{j_*\le j<k}K\bigl(2^{-j}(j+2)^2\bigr)^\alpha\\ &\quad+K\delta_n^{-2} \prod_{j_*\le j\le N_n}K\bigl(2^{-j}(j+2)^2\bigr)^\alpha +o(1). \end{aligned} \label{loc:failure-bound}$$ Here $j_*$ and the constants are independent of $J,n$ and of the unbounded part of the domain. In particular, the failure probability tends to zero by first taking $J$ large, then $\rho$ small at that stage, and finally the mesh sufficiently small.
  - Explanation: The failure probability of the shielding procedure is bounded by a head term, a sum over first large gate levels of products of arm bounds, and a final lattice-scale term, and tends to zero in the stated order of limits.
- **claim-reduction-bounded-dobrushin** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-reduction)): From every admissible approximation sequence one can extract a further subsequence and perform finite edge revelations with success probability tending to one, so that on success:* 1. *the unqueried connecting domain $\widehat\Omega_n$ is a Dobrushin polygon on the original mesh with the same thermal parameters and diameter at most $2S+20$;* 2. *conditional on the complete revelation record, its interface has the ordinary Dobrushin FK law;* 3. *every deterministic sequence of possible successful outputs $(\widehat\Omega_n,\widehat a_n,\widehat b_n)$ converges in the marked Carathéodory sense to $(\Omega,a,b)$;* 4. *the normalized coordinates on the retained domain are uniformly $o(1)$ from its original normalized coordinates, including prime-end occurrences, and the removed endpoint heads have vanishing disc diameter.* *All error bounds are uniform over successful outcomes.
  - Explanation: Finite edge revelations with success probability tending to one reduce any admissible approximation to a bounded Dobrushin polygon with the ordinary conditional law and normalized coordinates close to the original.
- **claim-universality-without-diameter** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-universality)): For $m>0$, every approximation sequence in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> converges to the law of Definition <a href="#def:law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:law">3</a>, in the specified normalized-disc curve topology.
  - Explanation: For positive mass every approximation sequence of the main theorem, with no diameter assumption, converges to the law of the continuum definition in the normalized-disc topology.
- **claim-chart-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-chart-independence)): The choice of $\phi$ is immaterial. Replacing it by $\lambda\phi$, $\lambda>0$, replaces $t,W_t,v$ by $\lambda^2t,\lambda W_t,\lambda v$. Directly from Equation <a href="#mod:C" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:C">[mod:C]</a>, the new coefficient at time $\lambda^2t$ is $C_t/\lambda$. Brownian scaling therefore preserves Equation <a href="#mod:SDE" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:SDE">[mod:SDE]</a> and its finite-energy condition. The initial disc normalization similarly provides coordinates for the curve topology, rather than extra data for the law.
  - Explanation: Replacing the half-plane chart by a positive multiple rescales time, driver and chart variable consistently. Brownian scaling preserves the driving equation and its finite-energy condition, so the law does not depend on the chart.
- **claim-actual-arm-scope** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-actual-arm)): The noncircular-bank hypothesis is a statement about the accessible side of the bank. A vertex identified with another occurrence on the far side of a slit does not supply an accessible wire of color $c$. One first works in incident sectors and then makes the monotone identification in the proof. Likewise an incident determined diagonal of color $c$ would expose its own color on an accessible bank; it cannot be silently placed on a bank declared to have the other color. Buffering by more than the diameter of the local cell templates removes the only ambiguity at the circular ends.
  - Explanation: The noncircular-bank hypothesis of the arm comparison concerns only the accessible side of a bank. Identifications across a slit do not supply an accessible wire of the arm color.
- **claim-filtration-measurability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-filtration)): The scalar field $h_t$ and its square derivative are functions of the current marked domain. Their interior data are kernel-continuous, and integrals of nonnegative functions of them are measurable by compact exhaustion. A signed lift need not be selected as a continuous deterministic function of every possible curve; the joint-limit construction in Proposition <a href="#geo:martingale" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:martingale">14</a> is enough for stochastic calculus. The geometric estimates in Corollary <a href="#geo:restart" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:restart">13</a> are needed only in the natural driver filtration. Their unconditional integrability consequences remain valid on adjoining the signed field, without an assertion that arbitrary filtration enlargements preserve conditional crossing estimates.
  - Explanation: The scalar field and its derivative are functions of the current marked domain, so no continuous deterministic signed lift is needed; the joint-limit construction suffices for stochastic calculus.
- **claim-energy-scope** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eng-scope)): The estimate holds on the entire capacity half-line. Neither an exponential-martingale estimate nor any absolute continuity with a Brownian-driven law was used in obtaining it. Its geometric inputs remain uniform after live restarts, and every conformal-radius infimum was taken before swallowing. These two features are what make the estimate available for the subsequent potential calculus.
  - Explanation: The energy estimate holds on the whole capacity half-line and uses no exponential-martingale estimate or absolute continuity with a Brownian-driven law.
- **claim-localized-energy-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): The uniqueness argument localizes energy on finite capacity horizons. It needs neither a global Novikov condition nor a comparison of complete infinite-horizon curve laws. In particular, no assertion of mutual absolute continuity at infinite capacity is used in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: Uniqueness is argued by localizing energy on finite capacity horizons, with no global Novikov condition and no comparison of infinite-horizon curve laws.
- **claim-collar-applicability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-collars)): Both lemmas apply to the cell collars used below. In the initial disc, each retained cell sector has vanishing diameter by Lemma <a href="#geo:crosscut" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:crosscut">10</a>. A boundary cell is attached to the old boundary through its own sector. Filling behind a boundary pinch is covered by the first paragraph of Lemma <a href="#loc:obstructions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:obstructions">35</a>. The finite-arc boundary condition in that lemma is imposed only on the approximating polygons and their conformal images, not on the limiting domain $\Omega$.
  - Explanation: The conformal small-set lemma and the small-attachment lemma apply to the cell collars used in the localization, since each retained cell sector is attached to the old boundary through its own sector.

        "exact_quote": "Every limit of Proposition <a href=\"#geo:compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:compactness\">11</a> inherits the conditional crossing estimates needed below in its natural driving filtration. They hold after a live stopping time and after localization on a finite interval on which the relevant Loewner coordinates remain nondegenerate. In the following two estimates, reset capacity time to zero at the chosen live stopping time.*\n\n1.  *In the current half-plane chart, put the tip at $0$, rescale a given live point to modulus $1$, and write $x$ for its real part. If its imaginary part is small, so $x$ is near $1$ or $-1$, then $$\\mathbb P\\{\\text{the future trace visits }B(x,d)\\mid\\text{past}\\}\n     \\le Kd^\\alpha$$ for all sufficiently small $d$.*\n\n2.  *For a live physical point $w$, $$\\mathbb P\\left\\{\n       \\inf_{s\\text{ before swallowing}}\n       \\operatorname{crad}_{O_s}(w)\n          <\\varepsilon\\,\\operatorname{crad}_{O_0}(w)\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\text{past at the restart}\\right\\}\n     \\le K\\varepsilon^\\alpha .$$*\n\n*The assertions apply in particular at inverse elapsed-clock times and at first angular-level crossings used in Section <a href=\"#eng:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eng:section\">4</a>.         "exact_quote": "Every subsequential curve law of Proposition <a href=\"#geo:compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:compactness\">11</a> has a joint realization with a complex field $M_t(w)$, $w\\in O_t$, such that $$M_t(w)^2=D h_t(w),\\qquad\n \\bar\\partial M_t+\\mathrm im\\overline{M_t}=0.$$ The field is continuous locally in space and time before swallowing, and is smooth in its spatial variable. On every protected compact subset of the live domain, after localization, $M_t(w)$ is a continuous martingale in the joint natural filtration of the curve and field.         "exact_quote": "There are $a>0$, $K<\\infty$, and $d_*>0$ with the following properties.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-eng-reflection",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a chordal Loewner trace in $\\mathbb H$ with continuous driver, starting from $0$. Let its maps be $l_t$, so that $l_0$ is the identity. Start a point at $z_0=x+\\mathrm iX_0$ with $|z_0|=1$. There are absolute constants $c_0>0$ and $K<\\infty$ such that, if $0<d<1/8$, $X_0\\le c_0d$, and the trace avoids $B(x,d)$, then up to the point’s lifetime $$\\label{eng:avoidance-deterministic}\n \\sup_s\\frac{X_s}{X_0}\\le\\frac Kd,\n \\qquad\n \\int_0^{\\sigma_*}\\frac{X_s}{X_0}\\,\\mathrm ds\\le\\frac Kd.$$ Consequently $$\\label{eng:avoidance-square}\n \\int_0^{\\sigma_*}\\left(\\frac{X_s}{X_0}\\right)^2\\mathrm ds\n \\le\\frac K{d^2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-223.4"
      },

        "exact_quote": "For the $p$ in Lemma <a href=\"#eng:decay\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eng:decay\">21</a>, there are $\\epsilon>0$ and $K<\\infty$ such that, uniformly over every initial live point, $$\\label{eng:weighted-clock}\n \\mathbb E\\int_0^{\\sigma_*}X_s^{2p}\n \\left[\n   \\left(\\frac{\\sqrt{t(s)}}{r_s}\\right)^\\epsilon\n  +\\left(\\frac{r_s}{\\sqrt{t(s)}}\\right)^\\epsilon\n \\right]\\mathrm ds\\le K.$$ Here $t(s)$ is capacity elapsed from the chosen starting instant.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cal-driver",
        "exact_quote": "The subsequential driving process is a continuous semimartingale and, in the above joint filtration, satisfies $$\\mathrm dW_t=\\sqrt{\\frac{16}{3}}\\,\\mathrm dB_t+\n                \\frac{2\\pi}{3}C_t\\,\\mathrm dt,\n \\qquad\n \\int_0^T C_t^2\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty\\quad\\text{almost surely for every }T<\\infty,\n \\label{cal:driver-equation}$$ where $B$ is a Brownian motion and $$C_t=\\frac4\\pi\\int_{\\mathbb H}P(v)\\mu_t(v)\n              |\\nabla(h_t\\circ\\psi_t)(v)|\\,\\mathrm dA(v).\n \\label{cal:C-definition}$$ In particular, neither absolute continuity of the quadratic variation nor absolute continuity of the finite-variation part of $W$ is an assumption of the argument.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cal-driver",
          "exact_quote": "The subsequential driving process is a continuous semimartingale and, in the above joint filtration, satisfies $$\\mathrm dW_t=\\sqrt{\\frac{16}{3}}\\,\\mathrm dB_t+\n                \\frac{2\\pi}{3}C_t\\,\\mathrm dt,\n \\qquad\n \\int_0^T C_t^2\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty\\quad\\text{almost surely for every }T<\\infty,\n \\label{cal:driver-equation}$$ where $B$ is a Brownian motion and $$C_t=\\frac4\\pi\\int_{\\mathbb H}P(v)\\mu_t(v)\n              |\\nabla(h_t\\circ\\psi_t)(v)|\\,\\mathrm dA(v).\n \\label{cal:C-definition}$$ In particular, neither absolute continuity of the quadratic variation nor absolute continuity of the finite-variation part of $W$ is an assumption of the argument.",
          "source_id": "PM-223.4"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cal-potential",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $K$, depending only on $m$ and a physical diameter bound, such that on every active domain $$\\begin{aligned}\n h_t(z)&=u_t(z)+4m\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)|M_t(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w),\n \\label{cal:representation}\\\\\n |M_t(w)|^2&\\leq \\frac{K}{\\operatorname{crad}_{O_t}(w)}.\n \\label{cal:bulk-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular the nonnegative potential $$Q_t(z):=\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)|M_t(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w)\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad 0\\leq Q_t(z)\\leq\\frac1{4m}.\n \\label{cal:bounded-potential}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-223.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cal-potential",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $K$, depending only on $m$ and a physical diameter bound, such that on every active domain $$\\begin{aligned}\n h_t(z)&=u_t(z)+4m\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)|M_t(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w),\n \\label{cal:representation}\\\\\n |M_t(w)|^2&\\leq \\frac{K}{\\operatorname{crad}_{O_t}(w)}.\n \\label{cal:bulk-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular the nonnegative potential $$Q_t(z):=\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)|M_t(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w)\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad 0\\leq Q_t(z)\\leq\\frac1{4m}.\n \\label{cal:bounded-potential}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-223.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a constant $K$, depending only on $m$ and a physical diameter bound, such that on every active domain $$\\begin{aligned}\n h_t(z)&=u_t(z)+4m\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)|M_t(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w),\n \\label{cal:representation}\\\\\n |M_t(w)|^2&\\leq \\frac{K}{\\operatorname{crad}_{O_t}(w)}.\n \\label{cal:bulk-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular the nonnegative potential $$Q_t(z):=\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)|M_t(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w)\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad 0\\leq Q_t(z)\\leq\\frac1{4m}.\n \\label{cal:bounded-potential}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cal-stochastic-potential",
        "exact_quote": "On every such stopped interval $[0,T]$, $$\\mathbb E\\int_0^T\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)\n                         \\,\\mathrm d[M(w),\\overline{M(w)}]_t\\,\\mathrm dA(w)<\\infty.\n \\label{cal:weighted-bracket}$$ The potential $Q_t(z)$ has a continuous semimartingale version, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathrm dQ_t(z)={}&-\\frac2\\pi P_t(z)\n       \\left(\\int_{O_t}P_t(w)|M_t(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w)\\right)\\mathrm dt\n \\notag\\\\\n &+\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)\\,\\mathrm d[M(w),\\overline{M(w)}]_t\\,\\mathrm dA(w)\n \\notag\\\\\n &+2\\operatorname{Re}\\int_{O_t}\n                G_t(z,w)\\overline{M_t(w)}\\,\\mathrm dM_t(w)\\,\\mathrm dA(w).\n \\label{cal:potential-differential}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last line is defined by the weighted bracket-isometry limit of compactly supported stochastic integrals. The statement holds simultaneously for countably many protected spatial points, and also as a spatial distribution identity on protected open sets.",

The statement holds simultaneously for countably many protected spatial points, and also as a spatial distribution identity on protected open sets.",

The statement holds simultaneously for countably many protected spatial points, and also as a spatial distribution identity on protected open sets.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cal-covariance",
        "exact_quote": "Locally in time there is a continuous increasing process $L$ such that $\\mathrm dt$, $|\\mathrm dV|$, $\\mathrm d[W^{\\mathrm M}]$, and all the spatial bracket measures used below are absolutely continuous with respect to $\\mathrm dL$. Outside one null set for $\\mathrm dL\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbb P$, write $$\\tau=\\frac{\\mathrm dt}{\\mathrm dL},\\qquad\n \\nu=\\frac{\\mathrm dV}{\\mathrm dL},\\qquad\n U=\\dot W^{\\mathrm M},\\qquad A_{\\mathrm{phys}}(w)=\\dot M(w).\n \\label{cal:clock-notation}$$ The dots denote covariance vectors with respect to $\\mathrm dL$, not time derivatives. These vectors lie in a real Hilbert space, complexified for $A_{\\mathrm{phys}}$. Their scalar products are the corresponding bracket densities. The product is extended bilinearly, so $A\\cdot\\overline A=|A|^2$ and $U\\cdot U=|U|^2$. The field $A_{\\mathrm{phys}}$ is spatially smooth on every compact subset of the live domain. Spatial evaluations, derivatives, compact tests, and the Green stochastic integrals in <a href=\"#cal:potential-differential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:potential-differential\">[cal:potential-differential]</a> have their covariance vectors on this same full set. In particular, for protected $z$, $$\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)|A_{\\mathrm{phys}}(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w)<\\infty\n \\quad\\text{for }\\mathrm dL\\text{-almost every live time.}\n \\label{cal:clock-green-integrability}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-223.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cal-covariance",
          "exact_quote": "Locally in time there is a continuous increasing process $L$ such that $\\mathrm dt$, $|\\mathrm dV|$, $\\mathrm d[W^{\\mathrm M}]$, and all the spatial bracket measures used below are absolutely continuous with respect to $\\mathrm dL$. Outside one null set for $\\mathrm dL\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbb P$, write $$\\tau=\\frac{\\mathrm dt}{\\mathrm dL},\\qquad\n \\nu=\\frac{\\mathrm dV}{\\mathrm dL},\\qquad\n U=\\dot W^{\\mathrm M},\\qquad A_{\\mathrm{phys}}(w)=\\dot M(w).\n \\label{cal:clock-notation}$$ The dots denote covariance vectors with respect to $\\mathrm dL$, not time derivatives. These vectors lie in a real Hilbert space, complexified for $A_{\\mathrm{phys}}$. Their scalar products are the corresponding bracket densities. The product is extended bilinearly, so $A\\cdot\\overline A=|A|^2$ and $U\\cdot U=|U|^2$. The field $A_{\\mathrm{phys}}$ is spatially smooth on every compact subset of the live domain. Spatial evaluations, derivatives, compact tests, and the Green stochastic integrals in <a href=\"#cal:potential-differential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:potential-differential\">[cal:potential-differential]</a> have their covariance vectors on this same full set. In particular, for protected $z$, $$\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)|A_{\\mathrm{phys}}(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w)<\\infty\n \\quad\\text{for }\\mathrm dL\\text{-almost every live time.}\n \\label{cal:clock-green-integrability}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-223.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Locally in time there is a continuous increasing process $L$ such that $\\mathrm dt$, $|\\mathrm dV|$, $\\mathrm d[W^{\\mathrm M}]$, and all the spatial bracket measures used below are absolutely continuous with respect to $\\mathrm dL$. Outside one null set for $\\mathrm dL\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbb P$, write $$\\tau=\\frac{\\mathrm dt}{\\mathrm dL},\\qquad\n \\nu=\\frac{\\mathrm dV}{\\mathrm dL},\\qquad\n U=\\dot W^{\\mathrm M},\\qquad A_{\\mathrm{phys}}(w)=\\dot M(w).\n \\label{cal:clock-notation}$$ The dots denote covariance vectors with respect to $\\mathrm dL$, not time derivatives. These vectors lie in a real Hilbert space, complexified for $A_{\\mathrm{phys}}$. Their scalar products are the corresponding bracket densities. The product is extended bilinearly, so $A\\cdot\\overline A=|A|^2$ and $U\\cdot U=|U|^2$. The field $A_{\\mathrm{phys}}$ is spatially smooth on every compact subset of the live domain. Spatial evaluations, derivatives, compact tests, and the Green stochastic integrals in <a href=\"#cal:potential-differential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:potential-differential\">[cal:potential-differential]</a> have their covariance vectors on this same full set. In particular, for protected $z$, $$\\int_{O_t}G_t(z,w)|A_{\\mathrm{phys}}(w)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(w)<\\infty\n \\quad\\text{for }\\mathrm dL\\text{-almost every live time.}\n \\label{cal:clock-green-integrability}$$",
      "explanation": "A single continuous increasing clock dominates time, the drift variation, the driver bracket and all spatial bracket measures, giving covariance vectors in a Hilbert space with a Green integrability property.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cal-tip-expansions",
        "exact_quote": "In the current conformal setting, assume <a href=\"#cal:mu-L2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:mu-L2\">[cal:mu-L2]</a>, <a href=\"#cal:weighted-A\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:weighted-A\">[cal:weighted-A]</a>, and the bulk bound $|F(v)|^2\\leq K/\\operatorname{Im}v$ from <a href=\"#cal:poisson-control\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:poisson-control\">[cal:poisson-control]</a>. Then <a href=\"#cal:identity-zero\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:identity-zero\">[cal:identity-zero]</a>–<a href=\"#cal:identity-drift\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:identity-drift\">[cal:identity-drift]</a> imply $$\\begin{aligned}\n F(z)^2&=\\frac1{\\pi z}+o(s^{-1})\n                   \\qquad\\text{uniformly on }B_s,\n \\label{cal:tip-leading}\\\\\n 2F(z)A(z)&=\\frac U{\\pi z^2}+o(s^{-3/2})\n                   \\qquad\\text{in }L^2_{\\rm av}(B_s),\n \\label{cal:tip-mixed-weak}\\\\\n \\int_{\\mathbb H}DG(z,v)\\mu(v)|A(v)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(v)\n             &=o(s^{-2})\n                   \\qquad\\text{in }L^1_{\\rm av}(B_s).\n \\label{cal:tip-bracket}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If in addition $J=\\int_{\\mathbb H}\\mu(v)|v|^{-2}\\,\\mathrm dA(v)<\\infty$, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n F(z)^2&=\\frac1{\\pi z}+C+o(1)\n                   \\qquad\\text{uniformly on }B_s,\n \\label{cal:tip-constant}\\\\\n 2F(z)A(z)&=\\frac U{\\pi z^2}+o(s^{-1})\n                   \\qquad\\text{in }L^2_{\\rm av}(B_s).\n \\label{cal:tip-mixed-strong}\n\\end{aligned}$$ No finiteness of $J$ is required for <a href=\"#cal:tip-leading\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:tip-leading\">[cal:tip-leading]</a>–<a href=\"#cal:tip-bracket\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:tip-bracket\">[cal:tip-bracket]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cal-tip-expansions",
          "exact_quote": "In the current conformal setting, assume <a href=\"#cal:mu-L2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:mu-L2\">[cal:mu-L2]</a>, <a href=\"#cal:weighted-A\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:weighted-A\">[cal:weighted-A]</a>, and the bulk bound $|F(v)|^2\\leq K/\\operatorname{Im}v$ from <a href=\"#cal:poisson-control\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:poisson-control\">[cal:poisson-control]</a>. Then <a href=\"#cal:identity-zero\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:identity-zero\">[cal:identity-zero]</a>–<a href=\"#cal:identity-drift\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:identity-drift\">[cal:identity-drift]</a> imply $$\\begin{aligned}\n F(z)^2&=\\frac1{\\pi z}+o(s^{-1})\n                   \\qquad\\text{uniformly on }B_s,\n \\label{cal:tip-leading}\\\\\n 2F(z)A(z)&=\\frac U{\\pi z^2}+o(s^{-3/2})\n                   \\qquad\\text{in }L^2_{\\rm av}(B_s),\n \\label{cal:tip-mixed-weak}\\\\\n \\int_{\\mathbb H}DG(z,v)\\mu(v)|A(v)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(v)\n             &=o(s^{-2})\n                   \\qquad\\text{in }L^1_{\\rm av}(B_s).\n \\label{cal:tip-bracket}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If in addition $J=\\int_{\\mathbb H}\\mu(v)|v|^{-2}\\,\\mathrm dA(v)<\\infty$, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n F(z)^2&=\\frac1{\\pi z}+C+o(1)\n                   \\qquad\\text{uniformly on }B_s,\n \\label{cal:tip-constant}\\\\\n 2F(z)A(z)&=\\frac U{\\pi z^2}+o(s^{-1})\n                   \\qquad\\text{in }L^2_{\\rm av}(B_s).\n \\label{cal:tip-mixed-strong}\n\\end{aligned}$$ No finiteness of $J$ is required for <a href=\"#cal:tip-leading\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:tip-leading\">[cal:tip-leading]</a>–<a href=\"#cal:tip-bracket\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:tip-bracket\">[cal:tip-bracket]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-223.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In the current conformal setting, assume <a href=\"#cal:mu-L2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:mu-L2\">[cal:mu-L2]</a>, <a href=\"#cal:weighted-A\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:weighted-A\">[cal:weighted-A]</a>, and the bulk bound $|F(v)|^2\\leq K/\\operatorname{Im}v$ from <a href=\"#cal:poisson-control\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:poisson-control\">[cal:poisson-control]</a>. Then <a href=\"#cal:identity-zero\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:identity-zero\">[cal:identity-zero]</a>–<a href=\"#cal:identity-drift\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:identity-drift\">[cal:identity-drift]</a> imply $$\\begin{aligned}\n F(z)^2&=\\frac1{\\pi z}+o(s^{-1})\n                   \\qquad\\text{uniformly on }B_s,\n \\label{cal:tip-leading}\\\\\n 2F(z)A(z)&=\\frac U{\\pi z^2}+o(s^{-3/2})\n                   \\qquad\\text{in }L^2_{\\rm av}(B_s),\n \\label{cal:tip-mixed-weak}\\\\\n \\int_{\\mathbb H}DG(z,v)\\mu(v)|A(v)|^2\\,\\mathrm dA(v)\n             &=o(s^{-2})\n                   \\qquad\\text{in }L^1_{\\rm av}(B_s).\n \\label{cal:tip-bracket}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If in addition $J=\\int_{\\mathbb H}\\mu(v)|v|^{-2}\\,\\mathrm dA(v)<\\infty$, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n F(z)^2&=\\frac1{\\pi z}+C+o(1)\n                   \\qquad\\text{uniformly on }B_s,\n \\label{cal:tip-constant}\\\\\n 2F(z)A(z)&=\\frac U{\\pi z^2}+o(s^{-1})\n                   \\qquad\\text{in }L^2_{\\rm av}(B_s).\n \\label{cal:tip-mixed-strong}\n\\end{aligned}$$ No finiteness of $J$ is required for <a href=\"#cal:tip-leading\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:tip-leading\">[cal:tip-leading]</a>–<a href=\"#cal:tip-bracket\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:tip-bracket\">[cal:tip-bracket]</a>.",

        "anchor": "stmt-loc-remote",
        "exact_quote": "For every connected interior arc $E_n\\subset\\Omega_n\\setminus\nB(0,S-1)$, its disc image has diameter tending to zero, uniformly over the choice of arc. The endpoints and shadows are understood in the prime-end compactification.         "anchor": "stmt-loc-obstructions",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V_n\\subset\\mathbb D$ be the simply connected component containing $0$ after removing finitely many closed, piecewise analytic obstructions and filling the other components. Assume that the obstructions lie within distance $\\varepsilon_n\\to0$ of $\\partial\\mathbb D$, and that every point on an obstruction can be joined to $\\mathbb C\\setminus\\mathbb D$ within the removed set by a connected set of diameter at most $\\varepsilon_n$.         "anchor": "stmt-loc-gates",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U_{n,j}$ be the component of $\\Omega_n\\cap B(0,s_j)$ containing $0$. Its visible exit arcs on $\\partial B(0,s_j)$ are crosscuts of the initial domain, with pairwise disjoint noncentral shadows. Every point of $\\Omega_n$ outside that circle lies behind one mandatory visible gate: every path from $0$ to that point crosses it. A gate at a later level has one specified ancestor gate at every earlier level.*\n\n*Call a gate at level $j$ large when its length is at least $2^{-j}$. There are at most $K_S2^j$ large gates at that level. A chain of small ancestors at levels $j$ gives disjoint annuli centered on those ancestors, with inner radii $K2^{-j}$ and outer radii $c_0(j+2)^{-2}$, for all sufficiently large $j$. Every path from a circle-$S$ portal to a point of radius $S+2$, continued to $0$ within $B(0,S)$, crosses these annuli.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-heads",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently small parameter tolerance $a>0$, the two ends can be explored, each stopped before its first unpeeled cell on a protected fence, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *Each explored head has disc diameter at most $Ka+o(1)$. The two heads lie in disjoint endpoint neighborhoods.*\n\n2.  *The center has unqueried connected access chains to the two resulting front corners. These chains lie in $B(0,S-1)$ and on the inner side of every proposed small $H$-cap and of the circle at $S$.*\n\n3.  *Conditional on these local revelations, the connecting problem is an ordinary Dobrushin problem. In the initial disc its changes are attached obstructions of diameter at most $Ka+o(1)$, with fillings behind them.*\n\n*The preimages of the $H$-cap cuts can subsequently be chosen to have any prescribed small physical diameter $\\rho>0$, while retaining these properties and containing the large gates at levels $j\\le J$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-shield",
        "exact_quote": "Let a distal cap have boundary color $c$, with its portal strictly inside the physical cutoff $S+3$. The new retained boundary is made of $c$-open diagonals attached to the original $c$-bank. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **positive-mass interface law** ([source](#stmt-def-law)): A positive-mass interface law in $(\Omega,a,b)$ is a law of an oriented continuous curve in the prime-end compactification, from $a$ to $b$, with the following properties.
- **isoradial mesh geometry** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Every primal face has circumradius $\delta$; the dual vertices are the circumcentres.
- **nome sequence** ([source](#mod:thermal-angle)): Choose any nome sequence $r_\delta\in(0,1)$ such that $r_\delta/\delta\to \|m\|/2$, and define $k_\delta\in(0,1)$ by $$r_\delta=\exp\left\{-\pi\frac{K(\sqrt{1-k_\delta^2})} {K(k_\delta)}\right\}.$$
- **Dobrushin boundary wiring** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Wire the counterclockwise boundary arc $(b^\delta a^\delta)$ by primal-open edges and $(a^\delta b^\delta)$ by dual-open edges.
- **thermal massive SLE** ([source](#stmt-thm-main)): In either case we call it thermal massive $\mathrm{SLE}_{16/3}$.
- **complex derivative operator D** ([source](#mod:square-gradient)): We write $D=2\mathrm i\partial_z$.
- **Loewner chart** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Choose a conformal chart $\phi:\Omega\to\mathbb H$ with $\phi(a)=0$ and $\phi(b)=\infty$.
- **arm** ([source](#geo:section)): An arm always means a path of actual sampled open edges of the indicated color.
- **Dirichlet Green kernel** ([source](#cal:green-normalization)): We use the Dirichlet Green kernel for $-\Delta$:
- **point lifetime** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Its lifetime $T_w$ is the supremum of the capacity times for which $w$ belongs to the active domain $O_t$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-diffusion-coefficient | diffusion coefficient of the driver | = | $\sqrt{\frac{16}{3}}$ | positive mass |
| q-drift-coefficient | drift prefactor multiplying the potential functional | = | $\frac{2\pi}{3}$ | positive mass |
| q-drift-integral-prefactor | normalizing prefactor of the drift functional | = | $\frac4\pi$ | positive mass |
| q-bvp-coupling | coefficient of the gradient nonlinearity in the scalar equation | = | $-4m$ | real mass |
| q-cluster-weight | cluster weight | = | $2$ | all admissible meshes |
| q-nome-ratio | ratio of nome to mesh | ~ | $\|m\|/2$ | mesh tending to zero |
| q-angle-bound | rhombus half-angle | ≤ | $\frac\pi2-\eta$ | a fixed positive angle margin independent of the mesh |
| q-scalar-range | scalar solution | ≤ | $1$ | unique bounded solution |
| q-energy-bound | expected integrated square of the moving-mark energy | ≤ | $K m^2\operatorname{area}(\Omega)$ | whole capacity half-line |
| q-moment-contraction | conditional small-power moment of the angular coordinate | ≤ | $K X_0^p e^{-bs}$ | clock times s at least zero |
| q-power-exponent-range | small power exponent | < | $1/4$ | positive exponent |
| q-conformal-radius-drop | conditional probability of a conformal radius drop | ≤ | $K\varepsilon^a$ | drop factor strictly between zero and one |
| q-supremum-tail | conditional tail of the supremum of the relative angular coordinate | ≤ | $Ku^{-a}$ | thresholds at least one |
| q-avoidance-supremum | supremum of the relative angular coordinate under ball avoidance | ≤ | $\frac Kd$ | small starting angle relative to the ball radius |
| q-avoidance-square-integral | integral of the squared relative angular coordinate under ball avoidance | ≤ | $\frac K{d^2}$ | small starting angle relative to the ball radius |
| q-green-potential-bound | Green potential of the squared fermion | ≤ | $\frac1{4m}$ | positive mass |
| q-fermion-bulk-bound | squared fermion magnitude | ≤ | $\frac{K}{\operatorname{crad}_{O_t}(w)}$ | constant depends on mass and diameter bound |
| q-green-kernel-variation | time derivative of the Green kernel | = | $-\frac2\pi P_t(z)P_t(w)$ | capacity time |
| q-bracket-density | squared norm of the driver covariance vector | = | $\frac{16}{3}\tau$ | clock times in the full set of the covariance lemma |
| q-large-gate-count | number of large gates at a radial level | ≤ | $K_S2^j$ | gates of length at least a dyadic threshold |
| q-shield-retained-radius | radius of retained parts of the cap on success | < | $S+3+K\delta_n$ | success of the exploration |
| q-shield-failure-radius | starting radius of the failure arm | ≥ | $S+3-K\delta_n$ | failure of the exploration |
| q-reduced-diameter | diameter of the retained Dobrushin polygon | ≤ | $2S+20$ | on success of the finite revelations |

They hold conditionally on the past at every stopping time $\\tau$ on the event $w\\in O_\\tau$.*\n\n1.  *Translate and dilate the current half-plane chart so that its tip is $0$, its target is $\\infty$, and $|Z_\\tau|=1$. If $X_\\tau\\le d_*$ and $x=\\operatorname{Re}(Z_\\tau/|Z_\\tau|)$, then, for $0<d<d_*$, the probability that the restarted trace visits $B(x,d)$ is at most $Kd^a$.*\n\n\n2.  *With $R=\\operatorname{crad}_{O_\\tau}(w)$ and $0<\\varepsilon<1$, $$\\label{eng:bulk-crad}\n     \\mathbb P\\left\\{\n       \\inf_{\\tau\\le t<T_w}\\operatorname{crad}_{O_t}(w)<\\varepsilon R\n          \\,\\middle|\\,\\mathcal F_\\tau\\right\\}\n     \\le K\\varepsilon^a.$$ The infimum in this formula is strictly before the lifetime. Conformal radius is not assigned the value zero upon swallowing.*\n\n*These estimates remain valid for the inverse-clock stopping times and the first angular-level stopping times used below.",
          "source_id": "PM-223.4"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-bvp-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-mod-bvp-unique) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mod-bvp-unique): Linearize the difference of two bounded solutions, build a bounded-drift killed diffusion by Girsanov, and conclude with optional stopping. -> uniqueness and continuity of the scalar solution
- **step-domain-markov** [claim](#stmt-geo-domain-markov) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-domain-markov): Pair medial half-edges locally, remove fixed pairings from the product weight, and clip along revealed diagonals. -> continuing Dobrushin problem with bounded conditional probabilities
- **step-arm-comparison** [claim](#stmt-geo-arm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-arm): Dominate any boundary partition by the fully wired one, couple by random-cluster monotonicity, and expose disjoint blocking circuits. -> power-law arm bound without multiplicity factor
- **step-g2** [claim](#stmt-geo-g2) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-g2): Show unforced components have one bank color, apply the arm comparison, and handle submesh scales with clearance templates. -> condition G2 uniformly
- **step-crosscut** [claim](#stmt-geo-crosscut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-crosscut): Length-area estimate on circular crosscuts, Koebe distortion, and polygon exhaustion. -> vanishing diameter in normalized coordinates
- **step-compactness** [claim](#stmt-geo-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-compactness): Apply the external compactness theory to the G2 condition and compare normalizations through converging disc automorphisms. -> tight laws with Loewner trace limits
- **step-active-domains** [claim](#stmt-geo-active) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-active): Kernel convergence of slit domains, then identify the moving prime end through a short connecting chain and the crosscut lemma. -> convergence of active domains and moving corner
- **step-restart-estimates** [claim](#stmt-geo-restart) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-restart): Transport discrete conditional estimates through stopped driver histories and pass to the limit with a monotone-class argument. -> conditional crossing estimates in the driver filtration
- **step-fermion-martingale** [claim](#stmt-geo-martingale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-martingale): Lift convergence of squares to signed martingales by excluding sheet switches, then pass martingale identities to the joint limit. -> continuous local martingale field
- **step-clock** [claim](#stmt-eng-clock) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eng-clock): Differentiate the imaginary part of the Loewner flow and the conformal radius, then change variables to the point clock. -> deterministic clock identities
- **step-restarted-estimates** [claim](#stmt-eng-restarts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eng-restarts): Half-annulus unforced crossing and Koebe comparison with rectangle crossings in blocking shells. -> restarted boundary and bulk estimates
- **step-reflection** [claim](#stmt-eng-reflection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eng-reflection): Schwarz reflection across the real axis, Koebe distortion, and monotonicity of the logarithmic derivative. -> deterministic angular bounds
- **step-angular-controls** [claim](#stmt-eng-controls) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eng-controls): Combine the reflected-ball bound with the boundary estimate for the tail and use the conformal radius identity for occupation tightness. -> supremum tail and occupation tightness
- **step-small-power-contraction** [claim](#stmt-eng-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eng-decay): Show strict contraction of a fractional moment at a deterministic clock time and iterate. -> exponential contraction of the small-power moment
- **step-weighted-clock** [claim](#stmt-eng-weighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eng-weighted): Bin by the running minimum of the angular coordinate and interpolate between the restarted bound and the contraction. -> uniform weighted clock integral
- **step-moving-mark-energy** [claim](#stmt-eng-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eng-weighted-2): Dyadic shell decomposition, weighted Cauchy–Schwarz, area change of variables and Tonelli. -> finite expected energy
- **step-potential-representation** [claim](#stmt-cal-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-potential): Interior gradient estimate and exhaustion with Green's formula. -> representation and bulk bound
- **step-stochastic-potential** [claim](#stmt-cal-stochastic-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-stochastic-potential): Insert height and compact cutoffs, apply the product formula, and remove cutoffs using the energy estimate. -> semimartingale differential of the Green potential
- **step-driver-semimartingale** [claim](#stmt-cal-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-recovery): Recover the driver by the implicit function theorem from potential differences at pairs of points with chart-change localization. -> semimartingale driver
- **step-covariance-clock** [claim](#stmt-cal-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-covariance): Realize the stopped fields as Hilbert-valued martingales and take operator densities with respect to a common trace clock. -> covariance vectors with respect to a single clock
- **step-tip-expansions** [claim](#stmt-cal-tip-expansions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-tip-expansions): Kernel bounds near and far from the shrinking disc, subharmonic mean inequalities and dominated convergence. -> averaged expansions near the moving mark
- **step-driver-identification** [claim](#stmt-cal-driver) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-tip-expansions-2): Compare cubic and quadratic poles, eliminate the singular clock part, and conclude by Brownian characterization. -> driver equation with finite energy
- **step-measurable-extension** [claim](#stmt-uni-measurable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uni-measurable): Exhaust the half-plane by compact sets and take a monotone limit of Borel functionals of the stopped driver. -> progressively measurable drift functional
- **step-weak-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-uni-weak) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uni-weak): Stop at energy levels, change to Wiener measure by Girsanov and write the stopped law through a fixed density. -> uniqueness in law
- **step-bounded-convergence** [claim](#stmt-uni-bounded) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uni-bounded): Tightness gives subsequential limits, the driver is identified, and uniqueness gives full-sequence convergence. -> unique law and bounded-domain convergence
- **step-remote-shadows** [claim](#stmt-loc-remote) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-remote): Restricted length-area estimate selects short preimage circles; connectedness traps remote images in small caps. -> vanishing diameter of remote pieces
- **step-small-obstructions** [claim](#stmt-loc-obstructions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-obstructions): Schwarz–Pick interior estimate plus a length-area crosscut argument at the boundary. -> uniform closeness of normalized maps
- **step-visible-gates** [claim](#stmt-loc-gates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-gates): Cut at circular arcs, obtain a finite tree of components, and read gates as root-adjacent edges. -> mandatory gates and ancestor annuli
- **step-endpoint-explorations** [claim](#stmt-loc-heads) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-heads): Choose endpoint fences with the remote-shadow lemma, protect access chains and explore up to the first protected cell. -> ordinary Dobrushin problem with small heads
- **step-exact-shielding** [claim](#stmt-loc-shield) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-shield): Search opposite-color clusters from exterior seeds, stop on reaching the portal buffer, and use the spatial Markov property on success. -> retained problem with ordinary law, or failure arm
- **step-failure-bound** [claim](#stmt-loc-failure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-failure): Union arm comparison per portal type, with products over disjoint annuli along ancestor gates. -> vanishing failure probability
- **step-reduction** [claim](#stmt-loc-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-reduction): Staged choice of tolerances, diagonal subsequence, and coordinate control through the obstruction lemma. -> bounded Dobrushin polygon with ordinary law
- **step-universality** [claim](#stmt-loc-universality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-universality): Integrate the uniform convergence of the bounded laws over the successful revelation records and use subsequences. -> universality without diameter assumption
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-37): Positive mass by universality; negative mass by duality, exchange of marks and path reversal. -> main convergence theorem

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-universal-limit**: For a bounded simply connected marked domain and a nonzero mass, the discrete thermal interface laws converge along the full mesh sequence to one curve law. That law does not depend on the lattices, the admissible domain approximations or the nome sequence.
- **inv-bvp-unique-bounded**: The scalar boundary value problem for the massive drift potential has exactly one bounded classical solution with the prescribed prime-end limits. The solution lies between zero and one and depends continuously on the marked domain on interior compact sets.
- **inv-weak-uniqueness-finite-energy**: For a fixed nonanticipating drift functional, at most one law of a continuous weak solution exists among solutions whose drift energy is almost surely finite on every finite horizon.
- **inv-driver-identification**: Every subsequential driving process in the bounded-diameter regime is a continuous semimartingale whose martingale part is Brownian with the stated variance and whose finite-variation part is absolutely continuous with the stated drift, with locally square-integrable coefficient.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-zero-mass**: Apply the massive drift and the limit law to the critical case or to a model with a different cluster weight. -> The identification of the driver relies on a massive fermionic observable with a nonzero mass; the argument gives no statement outside that setting.
- **anti-infinite-horizon-absolute-continuity**: Assume mutual absolute continuity of the massive law with the critical law on the whole capacity half-line and weight whole trajectories. -> Energy finiteness is only localized on finite capacity horizons; a global change-of-measure argument is not available.
- **anti-degenerate-or-nonisoradial-lattices**: Transfer the convergence theorem to planar graphs that are not isoradial or whose rhombus half-angles degenerate. -> The crossing and observable inputs are uniform only under the angle bound on isoradial rhombi.
- **anti-bounded-diameter-lemmas-without-localization**: Use the bounded-diameter compactness and crossing estimates directly on approximating domains of unbounded physical size. -> The constants depend on the diameter bound; for diverging diameters the localization procedure is required first.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are scaling-limit, compactness, identification and uniqueness theorems for random curves, proved by probabilistic and analytic arguments with limits, stopping times and existential extractions of subsequences. They do not state a finite procedure or algorithm with explicit inputs and outputs that a program could compute and an independent oracle could decide; the exploration steps are proof devices whose success is a probabilistic event, not a computable output.
